According to the Resolution to the Provision for General Reserve adopted in the 1st extraordinary shareholders' meeting of 2008 and the Resolution to the Preliminary Profit Distribution Plan of 2007 adopted in the shareholders' meeting of 2007, the Company provided 10% of the after-tax net profit of 341.09 million yuan, or 34.11 million yuan, as the statutory surplus reserve, provided 10% of the stock capital of 1,023,180,000 yuan, or 102.32 million yuan, as dividend to shareholders and provided general reserve of 193.19 million yuan.

2. According to the Resolution to Share Registration and Trusteeship of the Company adopted in the 1st extraordinary shareholders' meeting of 2008, the Company entrusted Zhejiang Property and Stock Exchange Co., Ltd. to register and deposit the shares of the Company. During the reporting period, 1835 corporate shareholders and natural–personal shareholders finished shareholding confirmation. 1,018,657,989 shares were confirmed, accounting for 99.56% of the total shares.

3. According to the Resolution to the Development for 2008–2010 of Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. adopted in the shareholders' meeting of 2007, the Company carried out the scientific development idea in full scale, targeted to be an outstanding retail bank and promoted the steady and effective business development.

4. According to the Resolution to the Business Plan Fulfillment in 2007 and the Business Plan of 2008 adopted in the shareholders' meeting of 2007, the total assets of the Company as of the end of 2008 was 34.114 billion yuan, increasing 4.025 billion yuan, or 13.38%, compared with that at the beginning of the year; the total liabilities was 32.321 billion yuan, increasing 3.698 billion yuan, or 12.92%, compared with that at the beginning of the year. The Company fulfilled 170.46% of the deposit plan, 166.80% of the loan plan and 112.79% of the owners' equity plan.

5. According to the Resolution to the Final Accounting of 2007 and Financial Budget for 2008 adopted in the shareholders' meeting of 2007, the Company fulfilled 124.40% of the revenue plan for 2008, 130.75% of the expenditure plan, 112.57% of the comprehensive profit plan, and 112.79% of the before-tax book profit plan. The profit/capital ratio was 28.08%, increasing by 5.43% compared with that in the previous year.

##### (iii) Duty performance of specialized committees under the Board

During the reporting period, the specialized committees under the Board effectively carried out the management requirements of the detailed rules and formulated the yearly work plan, implemented the routine meeting system, submitted important matters to the Board for discussion, established effective corporate governance transmission system and played the function as a frontier body.

The Risk Management Committee of the Board held 5 meetings in total and discussed 51 proposals. It analyzed and assessed the risks of the Company on a regular basis and reminded and gave precautions for the risk control and management for various businesses. It effectively controlled and prevented risks, explored the total risk management strategy and played the role of risk controller and supervisor.

The Committee of Related Party Transaction Control of the Board held 5 meetings in total and discussed 10 proposals. It amended the Management Rules on Transactions with Related Parties, regulated the day-to-day management for transactions with related parties, and examined major transactions with related parties to ensure the Company's transactions with related parties equal, open and fair.

The Nomination and Compensation Committee of the Board held 3 meetings in total and discussed 5 proposals. It nominated the members of the top management of the Company, formulated and amended the assessment plan for the top management and kept optimizing the incentive and restrictive system of the Company.

The Strategic Development Committee of the Board held 2 meetings in total and discussed 5 proposals. It made the fourth 3-year development plan of the Company scientifically, studied the number of shares issued in IPO, researched the matters related to setting up branches out of Zhejiang, and prepared for the construction project of Longwan Building and the headquarters building of the Company. It played the role of a strategic leader.

The Audit Committee of the Board held 2 meetings in total and discussed 8 proposals. It instructed and carried out specialized case handling, urged the implementation of regulatory opinions, carried out specialized audit such as management of transactions with related parties and tried to improve the internal control management ability.



The Information Technology Committee of the Board held 3 meetings in total and discussed 5 proposals. It assessed the risks of the IT system of the Company and gave reasonable advice on the information system risk and the construction of the technical staff team. It also made the Three-Year Plan for the Development of IT Application matching the new three-year development plan. It effectively played the important role of information technology in risk management.

##### (iv) Studying and Training of the Board

The Company held IPO training for directors, supervisory directors, top management and shareholders holding more than 5% of the shares at Onehome Howard Johnson Hotel Wenzhou on January 15, 2008. The training invited Beijing Tianyin lawyers Office, Lixin CPAs and Guoxin Securities Co., Ltd. to explain the Company Act, the Securities Act, the New Accounting Principles and the problems to be concerned in the IPO of the Company. It improved the understanding of the directors and supervisory directors on the IPO of the Company.

The Company held training of 2008 for directors and supervisory directors at Onehome Howard Johnson Hotel Wenzhou on October 24, 2008. The training invited independent director Chen Chaohui to make a lecture titled the Global Financial Crisis and Its Influence on Banks. It improved the understanding of the directors and supervisory directors on the world financial environment, China's economic situation and macroeconomic policy.

## XIV. Preliminary Plan for Profit Distribution or Turning Capital Reserve to Increase Stock Capital

The 29th meeting of the 3rd Board discussed and adopted the Resolution to the Preliminary Profit Distribution Plan for 2008 on January 15, 2009. The meeting resolved that in distributing the profit of 2008, provided 10% of the net profit of 2008 audited by Lixin CPAs as statutory surplus reserve, 10% of the stock capital as dividend to shareholders, 1% of the increased amount of risky assets as provision for general risk. The remaining distributable profit was used to provide for discretionary surplus reserve.

According to the above resolution, after providing 10% of the net profit of 466,120,000 yuan in 2008, or 46,610,000 yuan, as statutory surplus reserve. The distributable profit is 419,510,000 yuan. Provide 10% of the stock capital, or 102,320,000 yuan, as dividend for shareholders. After providing for the dividend for shareholders, the undistributed profit is 317,190,000 yuan. The Bank should provide allowance for general risk of 27,840,000 yuan. After providing for the allowance for general risk, the undistributed profit was 289,350,000 yuan, which is provided for discretionary surplus reserve as the support for capital adequacy and the expansion of branches.

## II. Independent Opinions of the Supervisory Board on Related Matters

##### (i) Lawful Operation

During the reporting period, the Company run business and managed the Company lawfully. Corporate governance was further optimized. The decision-making process was legal and effectively. Business activities were in line with the Company Act of the People's Republic of China, the Act of Commercial Banks of the People's Republic of China and the Articles of Incorporation. No directors and members of the top management of the Company was found activities breaching laws, regulations or the Articles of Incorporation or any activities that may impair the interests of the Company and its shareholders.

#### (ii) Authenticity of Financial Statements

The annual financial report of the Company has been audited by Lixin CPAs Co., Ltd. The financial report fairly indicated the financial status as of December 31, 2008 and the business outcomes, cash flow and changes in owners' equity of the Company of 2008 in all material aspects.

#### (iii) Use of Fund Raised

During the reporting period, the Company did not raise any fund. In the previous reporting period, the Company raised RMB 550 million yuan of subordinated fixed-term bonds, all of which was used to supply the supplementary capital of the Company, increase capital adequacy ratio and improve risk-resisting ability and overall strength. The Company used the raised fund legally and in line with regulations. No activities violating laws and regulations or damaging the interests of the Company and the shareholders have been found.

##### (iv) Acquisition and Sales of Assets of the Company

During the reporting period, the Company did not have any major acquisition or sales of assets.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_368.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


##### (v) Transactions with Related Parties

During the reporting period, the transactions with related parties of the Company mainly involved with various on-balance-sheet and off-balance-sheet credit business. The Company did not have any major transactions with related parties during the reporting period. Common transactions with related parties were operated pursuant to the rules of the regulatory authority and the credit rules of the Company. No related parties had priority to other borrowers. The risk of transactions with related parties was controllable. The transaction behavior was reasonable and fair, meeting the requirements on credit approving conditions and loan issuing management. No behavior damaging the interests of the Company and the shareholders was found.

##### (vi) Internal Control

During the reporting period, the Company formulated relatively reasonable and effective internal control system, which is complied with the reality of the Bank. The system plays an important role in regulating financial accounting behaviors, improving accounting information quality, enhancing business management, controlling operating risk, avoiding flaws and preventing malpractice. It guarantees the Bank's assets safe and integral, protects the rights and interests of interested parties related to the Bank, and improves the credit and market competitive strength of the Bank. Lixin CPAs Co., Ltd. audits the notes to the effectiveness of the internal control of the Company set in line with the Guideline for Internal Control Audit. Lixin CPAs certified that the criteria on the notes to the effectiveness of the internal control set by the Company kept effective internal control related to the financial statement in all material aspects as of December 31, 2008.

##### (vii) Implementation of Resolutions of Shareholders' Meeting

The Supervisory Board of the Company supervised the implementation of the 21 resolutions adopted by the shareholders' meeting. Nine resolutions had been carried out. As to the other 12 resolutions concerning the listing and related systems, the Company had prepared for the IPO. The resolutions will be carried out after the Company is listed on the stock market. The Supervisory Board had no objection against all reports and proposals submitted by the Board to the Shareholders' Meeting during the reporting period.

## ☐ XI. Important Matters

## I. Subordinated Bond Issued by the Company on the Interbank Bond Market

According to the Administrative Licensing Letter of the People's Bank of China (YSCZYZ [2007] No.24) of the People's Bank of China and the Approval of Subordinated bond Issuance of Wenzhou City Commercial Bank of China Banking Regulatory Commission (YJF [2007] No.278), the Company raised subordinated bonds of RMB 550 million yuan. The term is 10 years (at the end of the fifth year, the issuer has the right to choose to redeem the bonds). The issuing price is fixed annual interest rate of 5.5%. The interest is paid annually.

## II. Important Litigation and Arbitration

As of December 31, 2008, the Company had 3 cases involving object of action amounting to RMB 10 million yuan (incl.). The total principal of the objects was RMB 45,128,300 yuan. The details are as follows:

## III. Acquisition and Sale of Assets, Takeover and Merger

During the reporting period, the Company did not acquire or sell any asset or take over or merge with any company.

##### (i) Major transactions with related parties

## VI. Transactions with Related Parties

Major transactions with related parties disclosed by the Company refer to a single transaction with a related party of the Company and the amount of the transaction is over 1% of the Company's equity, or after the Company finishes a transaction with a related party, the balance of the transaction with the related party accounts for over 5% of the Company's equity.

As of the end of the reporting period, the equity of the Company was 2,412,938,200 yuan. The Company did not have any major transactions with related parties.

##### (ii) Ordinary transactions with related parties

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_367.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


Ordinary transactions with related parties disclosed by the Company refer to a single transaction with a related party of the Company and the amount of the transaction is below 1% of the Company's equity, or after the Company finishes a transaction with a related party, the balance of the transaction with the related party accounts for below 5% of the Company's equity.

As of the end of 2008, the Company had ordinary transactions with 208 related parties with a loan balance of RMB 243,242,300 yuan, in which, transactions with directors, supervisory directors, top management and their relatives amounted to 11,515,000 yuan, accounting for 99.06% of the mortgaged loans. The mortgage procedures were complied with laws and regulations, without any preferential condition to that of other borrowers. The risk was controllable and the transaction behavior was proper and fair. The transactions were complied with laws and regulations, meeting the Company's credit approving conditions and requirements on loan issuing management.

##### (iii) All Related Level

As of the end of the reporting period, the Company's transactions with related parties were RMB 243,242,300 yuan in total. After deducting the amount of pledge deposits and loans secured by deposit certificates, the transactions with related parties accounted for 9.23% of the equity at the end of 2008, complying with the requirements of the regulatory authority.

## V. Major Contracts and Implementation of Major Contracts

##### (i) Major trusteeship, contracting and lease

To regulate stock ownership management, the Company entrusted Zhejiang Property and Stock Exchange Co., Ltd. for centralized trusteeship in March 2008.

During the reporting period, the Company never had any major contracting and lease.

#### (ii) Major guarantees

During the reporting period, the Company did not have any major guarantees that needed to disclose except for the financial guarantee business within the business scope approved by the China Banking Regulatory Commission.

##### (iii) Other major contracts (including guarantees) and implementation of the contracts

During the reporting period, the Company had 2 non-loan contracts of which the amount of the object was over RMB 50 million yuan (incl.). The total amount of the subject matters was RMB 147,515,800 yuan, in which, the subject matter of the construction contract of Longwan Branch Building was 54,513,400 yuan and the subject matter of the property purchase and sale contract of Ningbo Branch Building was 93,002,400 yuan.

## VI. Hiring and Dismissing Certified Public Accountants

During the reporting period, the Company hired Lixin Certified Public Accountants Co., Ltd. to be external auditor of 2008.

## VII. Penalties on the Company, Board of Directors and Directors

During the reporting period, some sub-branches of the Company were imposed fines of 360,000 yuan by China Banking Regulatory Commission Wenzhou Branch due to behaviors of violation.

## VIII. Writing off bad loans during the reporting period

During the reporting period, the Company wrote off 25 bad loans, amounting to 10,380,700 yuan. It further improved the risk-resisting ability and market competitiveness. The details are as follows:

In June 2008, upon the approval of the state tax administration, the Company wrote off 14 bad loans, amounting to 7,513,500 yuan.

In November 2008, upon the approval of the state tax administration, the Company wrote off 11 bad loans, amounting to 2,867,200 yuan.

# 立信會計師事務所有限公司 Shu Lun Pan Certified Public Accountants Co., Ltd.

Auditors' Report

XKSBZ (2009) No.22121

All Shareholders of Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd.:

We have audited the attached financial statements of Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. (hereinafter the Bank), including the Balance Sheet as of December 31, 2008, the Profit Statement, the Statement on Changes in Shareholders' Equity, the Cash Flow Statement and Notes to Financial Statements of 2008.

1. Responsibilities of the management for the financial statements

## II. Responsibilities of the CPAs

It is the responsibility of the management of the Bank to prepare these financial statements according to the Enterprise Accounting Principles. The responsibilities include: (1) to design, carry out and maintain the internal control related to the preparation of the financial statements in order to ensure the financial statements free of material errors caused by fraud or mistakes; (2) to choose and adopt proper accounting policy; and (3) to make reasonable accounting estimates.

We take the responsibility to give our opinions based on the audit of the financial statements. We carried out the audit in accordance with the China CPAs Auditing Principles, which requires us to observe the professional ethics and rules in planning and carrying out the audit so as to obtain proper evidence indicating if there is any material error in the financial statements.

Our audit involved in carrying out the audit procedures in order to obtain the evidence supporting the amounts and disclosures in the financial statements. The selection of the audit procedures was depended on the judgment of the CPAs. The judgment included the evaluation on the risks of material errors in the financial statements caused by fraud or mistakes. During the risk evaluation, we considered the internal control related to the preparation of the financial statements so as to design proper audit procedures; however, the purpose was not to give our opinions on the effectiveness of the internal control. Our audit also included evaluating the rightness of the accounting policy and the rationality of the accounting estimates made by the management as well as evaluating the overall properness of the financial statements.

We believe that the audit evidence we obtained was complete and proper, which has provided reasonable basis for the auditor's opinions.

##### Ⅲ. Auditors' Opinions

In our opinion, the financial statements of the Bank were complied with the requirements of Enterprise Accounting Principles and have fairly indicated the financial position as of December 31, 2008 and the business outcomes and cash flow in 2008 of the Bank in all material aspects.

Shu Lun Pan Certified Public Accountants Co., Ltd

President: Xing Zengfu

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2318_199_2384_369.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


Person in charge of the accounting department: Zhang Demin

Person in charge of accounting: Jin Jiankang

# Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. Notes to Financial Statements of 2008

### 1. General Information

#### (i) History

Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. (the "Bank") is a joint-stock commercial bank founded pursuant to the policy in the Notice of Establishing City Cooperative Banks (GF [1995] No.25) published by the State Council and upon the Approval of the Opening of Wenzhou City Commercial Bank (YF [1998] No.395) issued by the People's Bank of China. The Bank received Business License for Enterprise as Legal Person numbered 3300001005495 issued by Zhejiang Provincial Administration for Industry and Commerce on March 10, 1999.

The former name of the Bank was Wenzhou City Commercial Bank Co., Ltd., which was renamed Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. in line with the Approval of Change of Name of Wenzhou City Commercial Bank YJF (2007) No.375) issued and approved by China Banking Regulatory Commission on September 6, 2007. The Bank received Business License for Enterprise as Legal Person issued by Zhejiang Provincial Administration for Industry and Commerce on September 25, 2007 after the change of name.

The Bank had a registered capital of RMB 290,495,800 yuan upon incorporation. In February 2004, the Bank increased registered capital for the first time, increasing RMB 180,300,000 yuan. The registered capital was changed to 470,795,800 yuan. In June 2006, the Bank increased registered capital for the second time, in which 50,233,900 yuan was transferred from capital reserve and undistributed profit, and 486,183,800 yuan was newly invested by 17 enterprise corporate shareholders and 257 employee shareholders. The registered capital was changed to 1,007,213,500 yuan. In December 2006, the Bank increased registered capital for the third time by increasing RMB 15,968,400 yuan transferred from undistributed profit. The registered capital was changed to RMB 1,023,181,900 yuan. As of December 31, 2008, the biggest shareholder of the Bank was China Huarong Assets Management Company, holding 8.17% of the shares of the Bank.

As of December 31, 2008, the registration number of the Business License for Enterprise as Legal Person of the Bank was 330000000008227 and the registration number of the License for Financial Business Operation was B0153H233030001. The legal representative of the Bank was Xia Ruizhou and registered address 1# Building, Huahai Plaza, Chezhan Avenue, Wenzhou.

#### (ii) Setup of Business Organizations

As of December 31, 2008, the Bank had a Headquarters and 61 branches. They are the Headquarters, Ningbo Branch, Quzhou Branch, Yueqing Sub-branch, Rui'an Sub-branch, Pingyang Sub-branch, Yongjia Sub-branch, Liushi Sub-branch, Tangxia Sub-branch, Hongqiao Sub-branch, Lucheng Sub-branch, Ouhai Sub-branch, Longwan Sub-branch, Guoding Sub-branch, Fuqian Sub-branch, Jinying Sub-branch, Fulong Sub-branch, Desheng Sub-branch, Huihai Sub-branch, Puxieshi Sub-branch, Da'nan Sub-branch, Liming Sub-branch, Huajian Sub-branch, Laowu Sub-branch, Qinfen Sub-branch, Wutian Sub-branch, Quxi Sub-branch, Zhuangyuan Sub-branch, Shifulu Sub-branch, Cangnan Sub-branch, Hongchang Sub-branch, Chengdong Sub-branch, Shizhong Sub-branch, Railway Station Sub-branch, Dengfeng Sub-branch, Yinxin Sub-branch, Yongxing Sub-branch, Xincheng Sub-branch, CBD Sub-branch, Zhanqian Sub-branch, Xinqiao Sub-branch, Shuanglong Sub-branch, Chengnan Sub-branch, Shunjing Sub-branch, Qinye Sub-branch, Nanpu Sub-branch, Lizhong Sub-branch, Chengxi Sub-branch, Xueyuanlu Sub-branch, Xincheng Sub-branch, Jiefanglu Sub-branch, Jiangbin Sub-branch, Baili Sub-branch, Jingshan Sub-branch, Development Zone Sub-branch, Haicheng Sub-branch, Binhai Sub-branch, Huanglong Sub-branch, Guoxi Sub-branch, Kunyang Sub-branch, and Wencheng Sub-branch.



On October 20, 2008, upon the approval of File WYJB [2008] No.299 by China Banking Regulatory Commission Wenzhou Regulatory Bureau, the Bank established Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. Wencheng Subbranch on October 21, 2008.

On August 29, 2008, upon the approval of File ZYJF [2008] No.430 by China Banking Regulatory Commission Zhejiang Regulatory Bureau, the Bank established Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. Ningbo Branch on December 29, 2008.

#### (iii) Main business and service provided

As of December 31, 2008, the main businesses of the Bank approved by China's People's Bank and China Banking Regulatory Commission were as follows: accepting public deposits; issuing short-term, mid-term and long-term loans; handling domestic and foreign settlements; bill cashing and discounting; issuing financial bonds, entrusted to issue, cash and underwrite governmental bonds; buying and selling governmental bonds and financial bonds; inter-bank funds lending; operating bankcard business; providing L/C service and guarantee; authorized to collect and pay money or authorized to handle insurance services; providing safe deposit box service; self-operated foreign exchange trade and trade on behalf of others; personal foreign exchange trade business; letter of indemnity service; other businesses approved by the banking regulatory authority of the State Council.

## II. Main Accounting Policies and Accounting Estimates

#### (i) Statement on following Enterprise Accounting Principles

The financial statements and notes to the financial statements were released on March 20, 2009 upon the approval of the Board of the Bank.

The financial statements are complied with the requirements of Note II (ii) Basis for Preparation of Financial Statements below and truly and completely reflect the financial position, business outcome and cash flow of the Bank.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_368.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


### (ii) Basis for preparation of financial statements

On the basis of constant business operation and according to the actually occurred transactions and events, the Bank follows Enterprise Accounting Principles— Basic Principles and other accounting principles (hereinafter the new principles or the new accounting principles) published by the Ministry of Finance in February 2006 to recognize and measure transactions and events, and prepare financial statements based on that.

### (iii) Fiscal period

The fiscal year of the Company is from January 1 to December 31, on the Gregorian calendar.

#### (iv) Currency for accounting

The Company uses RMB as the currency for accounting

#### (v) Nature of accounting

The Bank adopts accrual basis accounting system for accounting recognition, measurement and reporting. Except for fair value, net cash realizable value and present value involved in the Notes, the others are recorded at their historical costs.

The nature of accounting of the reported items in the reporting period did not have any changes.

#### (vi) Cash and cash equivalents

Cash refers to cash held by the Bank and deposits that can be paid at any time, including cash held by the Bank, non-restricted deposits for payment that placed in the central bank, deposits on call placed in other banks. Cash equivalents are investment of short term, high liquidity, easy to convert to cash of known amount, and very low risk of value change, including deposits placed in other banks, bond investment and buying securities under agreements to resell that will be mature in three months.

#### (vii) Accounting for transactions in foreign currencies

Transactions in foreign currencies are accounted separately according to different currencies. At the end of the period, prepare financial statements in RMB. The principle for preparing financial statements in RMB is: prepare financial statements in each currency. Then convert the amounts in various currencies in USD. Then, convert USD to RMB and consolidate the statements to a financial statement in RMB.

At the balance date, currency items in foreign currency are converted to RMB at the exchange rate at the balance date. The difference of foreign exchanged caused enters into the gain or loss account of the period. Non-monetary items in foreign currency are converted at the exchange rate of the original transaction date. Non-monetary items in foreign currency are converted to RMB at the exchange rate of the day confirming the fair value. As to the difference between the converted amount of RMB and the original amount of RMB, if it belongs to non-monetary item in foreign currency of available-for-sale financial assets, the difference enters into capital reserve account. Differences of other items enter into the gain or loss account of the period.

### (viii) Financial assets and financial liabilities

## 1. Categorization, recognition and accounting of financial assets

According to the purpose of acquiring financial assets, the Bank classifies financial assets held to the following four categories: financial assets recorded at their fair value and the changes in fair value enter into the gain or loss account of the period, including tradable financial assets and financial assets appointed to record at their fair value and of which the changes enter into the gain or loss of the period; held-to-maturity investment; loans and receivables; available-for-sale investments. Financial assets are recorded at their fair value upon the initial recognition. As to the financial assets which do not belong to the category of financial assets recorded at fair value and their changes entering into the gain or loss of the period, the related transaction expense directly attributable to the acquisition of the financial assets should be added in the initial recognition.

### (1) Financial asset recorded at fair value with its changes entering gain or loss of the period

Financial assets at fair value and its changes through gain or loss of the period include financial assets held for trade or financial assets appointed by the management as the financial asset at fair value and its changes through gain or loss of the period at the initial recognition. The return on investment during holding such financial assets and the change in fair value recorded at the fair value at the end of the period enters into the gain or loss account of the period. In disposing such assets, the difference between its fair value and the original bookkeeping amount is recognized as return on investment. Meanwhile, adjust gain or loss caused by the change in fair value.

### (2) Held-to-maturity investment

Held-to-maturity investment refers to non-derivative financial assets with fixed or certain recoverable amount and fixed mature date and the Bank has clear intention and ability to hold to maturity. Held-to-maturity investment is recorded at the amount of amortized cost calculated with actual interest rate method deducting the allowance for devaluation of the investment. The gain or loss on a held-to-maturity investment caused by being stopped recognition, devalued or amortization enters into the gain or loss account of the period.

In the current fiscal period or the previous two fiscal years, if the Bank sells or re-categorizes high-amount held-to-maturity investment at the mature date (high amount refers to the total amount related to the investment before sale or re-categorization), then the Bank should not categorize any such financial assets to the category of held-to-maturity investment. Sales or re-categorization meeting the following conditions are exceptional: The selling date or re-categorizing date is near the mature date or redemption date (e.g., three months before maturity) of the investment so that the changes in the market interest rate have no significant influence on the fair value of the investment. The sale or re-categorization happens after recovering almost all initial principal of the investment according to the agreed repayment mode or early repayment mode; or the sale or re-categorization falls into some independent event that the Bank is unable to control and predict reasonably and the event will not happen again.

### (3) Loans and receivables

Loans and receivables are non-derivative financial assets that have fixed recoverable amount or certain recoverable amount and lack active market.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_367.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


The loans and receivables of the Bank mainly include loans issued, payments on other's behalf, deposits in the central bank and other banks, purchase of financial assets under agreements to resell and other receivables. The loans issued and the payments on other's behalf by the Bank include common loans, discount, payments on other's behalf (including payments for bank acceptance bill, payments for L/Cs, payments for guarantees), trade loans and overdraft of credit card.

Overdue loan: loan that cannot be paid back when it is due (including renewed term) because of the reasons of the borrower; or passive payment on behalf of others because the acceptor of the bill is unable to pay on time and the account balance of the discount applicant is insufficient; or passive payment on behalf of customers because the bill accepted by the Bank is mature but the account balance of the acceptance applicant is insufficient; or overdue import and export negotiation or off-balance-sheet payment on behalf of others such as L/C and guarantee, which is recognized as overdue loan from the day of paying the amount on behalf of others.

The value of loans and receivables is recorded at the amount of amortized cost calculated with actual interest rate method deducting the allowance for devaluation of the investment. The gain or loss caused by stopping recognition, devaluation or amortization of a loan or receivable enters into the gain or loss account of the period.

##### (4) Available-for-sale financial assets

Available–for–sale financial assets refer to non–derivative financial assets that are appointed as available for sale or investment not categorized to loans or receivables or held–to–maturity investment or financial assets at fair value of which the changes in fair value enter into the loss or gain account of the period. In subsequential accounting period, such financial assets are recorded at their fair value. The unrealized income caused by the changes in the fair value of an available–for–sale financial asset enters into capital reserve (other capital reserve) before the financial asset is stopped recognition or devalued. When the financial asset is stopped recognition or devalued, the accumulated changes in the fair value entered into capital reserve previously should be carried over to the gain or loss account of the period.

## 2. Classification, recognition and measurement of financial liabilities

According to the purpose of financial liabilities, the Bank classifies the financial liabilities held to the following categories: financial liabilities recorded at fair value and of which the changes enter into the gain or loss account of the period and other financial liabilities.

(1) Financial liabilities recorded at fair value and of which the changes enter into the gain or loss account of the period

The Bank classifies financial liabilities recorded at fair value and of which the changes enter into the gain or loss account of the period to two categories: financial liabilities held for trade, and financial liabilities appointed by the management as financial liabilities recorded at fair value and of which the changes enter into the gain or loss account of the period upon initial recognition. The original amount of such financial liabilities is recognized at their fair value. The related expenses enter into the gain or loss account of the period. The cost paid during the period holding the financial liabilities and all changes in the fair value recorded at fair value enter into the gain or loss account of the period. In disposing such liabilities, the difference between its fair value and the original bookkeeping amount is recognized as return on investment. Meanwhile, adjust gain or loss caused by the change in fair value.



### (2) Other financial liabilities

Except for financial liabilities held for trade or financial liabilities recorded at fair value and of which the changes in the fair value enter into the gain or loss account of the period, deposits from other banks and financial institutions, deposits received, sale of financial assets under agreement to repurchase, bond payables and other payables are other financial liabilities, of which the original amount is recognized at the sum of their fair value and related expense. In subsequentia accounting period, other financial liabilities are recorded at their amortized cost.

## 3. Recognition of the fair value of financial assets and financial liabilities

The fair value of the financial assets and financial liabilities of the Bank which are recorded at their fair value refers to the quotation of the active market.

## 4. Transferring and stopping recognition of financial assets and financial liabilities

### (1) Financial assets

When the contract for receiving the future cash flow of a financial asset expires, or the Bank has transferred almost all related risks and returns of the financial asset, or though the Bank doesn't transfer or keep almost all risks and returns related to the ownership of the financial asset, the bank has given up the control of the financial asset, then stop recognizing the financial asset.

When a financial asset is transferred, if almost all risks and returns of the financial asset is kept, do not stop recognizing the financial asset. If the Bank doesn't transfer or keep almost all risks and returns and doesn't give up the control of the financial asset, then recognized the financial asset according to the degree that the Bank involves in the transferred financial asset and recognize related liability.

### (2) Financial liabilities

When the present liability of a financial liability is fulfilled, expired or lifted partly or entirely, stop recognizing the financial liability entirely or partly of the financial liability.

### (ix) Long-term equity investment

Long-term equity investment is recorded at its original investment cost upon its acquisition.

If the Bank doesn't control, jointly control or have major influence on the company invested, the investment doesn't have a quotation in an active market and the fair value of the investment cannot be measured reliably, then the long-term equity investment is accounted with cost method. When cost method is adopted, long-term equity investment is recorded at its original investment cost. When the company invested declares distributing cash dividend or profit, the dividend or profit is recognized as the gain or loss of the investment of the period. The recognized return on investment is only limited to the distributed amount of the net profit balance after the company invested accepting the investment. If the amount of received profit or cash dividend exceeds the above amount, the exceeding amount is recognized as recovery of investment cost. In disposing long-term equity investment, the difference between its book value and the actually received amount enters into the gain.

## (x) Subsequential accounting for real properties as investment

Real properties as investment refer to real properties held for gaining rental or capital appreciation, or for both purposes.

The real properties as investment of the Bank are recorded with cost method subsequently.

Real properties purchased or built by the Bank are recognized at their cost as the initial amount. To a real property transferred from a fixed asset, the initial amount of the real property is the book value of the transferring date.

<div style="text-align: center;">Years of depreciation and annual depreciation rate of real properties as investment are as follows:</div>

## (xi) Accounting for fixed assets

## 1. Recognition of fixed assets

Fixed assets are tangible assets held for producing commodities, providing services, leasing or business management of which the service life is over one fiscal year. A fixed asset is recognized when it meets the following conditions at the same time:

(1) Financial income related to the fixed asset is likely to flow into the enterprise; and

(2) The cost of the fixed asset can be measured reliably.

## 2. Categories of fixed assets

Fixed assets are categorized to: buildings and constructions, tools and equipment, transportation equipment, electronic equipment, and improvement of fixed assets.

## 3. Original cost of fixed assets

The original value of fixed assets is its actual cost upon acquisition.

The cost of a purchased fixed asset is recognized at the sum of its purchasing price, related taxes and the freight, loading and unloading fee, installation fee and professional service fee of the asset before the asset reaches the planned useful status.

If the payment of the purchasing price of a fixed asset is postponed beyond normal credit conditions and virtually has financing nature, then the cost of the fixed asset is recognized at the present value of its purchasing price.

The cost of a fixed asset built by the Bank itself consists of the necessary cost occurred before the asset reaches the planned useful status.

In debt restructuring, if a fixed asset is acquired from debtor's offsetting the debt, the fixed asset is recognized at the fair value. The difference between the book value of the restructured debt and the fair value of the asset enters into the gain or loss account of the period.



Under the precondition when non-monetary asset swap is virtually commercial and the fair value of the asset swapped in or out can be measured reliably, the asset swapped in is recorded at the fair value of the asset swapped out unless there is sure evidence indicating the fair value of the asset swapped in is more reliable. In non-monetary asset swap that does not meet the above precondition, the cost of the fixed asset swapped in is recorded at the book value and the related tax payable of the asset swapped out and no gain or loss is recognized.

## 4. Method of depreciation on fixed assets

Depreciation on fixed assets is accounted on an average basis under straight-line method. The depreciation rate is determined according to the category of the fixed assets, the estimated service life and the estimated residual value rate.

The years of depreciation and the yearly depreciation rate of main fixed asset categories are as follows:


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Category</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Years of depreciation (years)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Residual value (%)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Annual depreciation rate (%)</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Buildings and constructions</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>40</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2.43</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Special equipment</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>5/8</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>0</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>20.00/12.50</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Transportation equipment</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>8/10</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>0</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>12.50/10.00</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>General equipment</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>5/10/20</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>0</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>20.00/10.00/5.00</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Other equipment</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>10</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>0</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>10.00</td></tr></table>

## (xii) Accounting for construction in progress

1. Categories of construction in progress

Construction in progress is recorded at its actual cost under each approved project.

2. Standards and time point of construction in progress being carried over to fixed assets

All cost of the construction in progress before the asset reaches the planned useful status is recognized as the book value of the fixed asset. When the construction in progress for a fixed asset has reached the planned useful status but does not finish final accounting procedures, from the day of the asset reaches the planned useful status, it shall be carried over to fixed asset at the estimated value according to the project budget, construction cost or actual project cost and provide depreciation on fixed assets according to the fixed assets depreciation policy of the Company. When the final accounting procedures are finished, adjust the estimated value according to the actual cost. However, the depreciation provided is not adjusted.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_367.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


### (Xiii) Accounting for intangible assets

Intangible assets are recorded at their original cost.

The cost of purchased intangible asset includes purchasing price, related taxes and other expenses directly for the asset reaching the planned purpose. If the payment of the purchasing price of an intangible asset is postponed beyond normal credit conditions and virtually has financing nature, then the cost of the intangible asset is recognized at the present value of its purchasing price.

In debt restructuring, if an intangible asset is acquired from debtor's offsetting the debt, the intangible asset is recognized at the fair value. The difference between the book value of the restructured debt and the fair value of the intangible asset enters into the gain or loss account of the period.

Under the precondition when non-monetary asset swap is virtually commercial and the fair value of the intangible asset swapped in or out can be measured reliably, the asset swapped in is recorded at the fair value of the asset swapped out unless there is sure evidence indicating the fair value of the asset swapped in is more reliable. In non-monetary asset swap that does not meet the above precondition, the cost of the intangible asset swapped in is recorded at the book value and the related tax payable of the asset swapped out and no gain or loss is recognized.

The cost of the intangible asset developed by the Bank itself includes: the materials consumed in developing the intangible asset, labor cost, registration fee, amortization of other patents and franchises used in the development process, interest expense which meets the condition for capitalization and other direct expenses for the intangible asset reaching the planned purpose.

## 2. Service life and amortization of intangible assets

All intangible assets of the Bank are intangible assets with limited service life. Since the month of acquiring the intangible asset, the intangible asset is amortized evenly under straight-line method within its service years. Software is amortized evenly in five years from the month of buying the software.

At the end of the each period, check the service life and amortization method of the intangible assets with limited service life.

According to the check result, the service life and amortization method of the intangible assets have no difference from the previous estimates.

### (xiv)Accounting for long-term prepaid expense

Long-term prepaid expense refers to various expenses that have been paid but the beneficial period is over 1 year (excl.). Long-term prepaid expense is recorded at its actual amount and amortized evenly throughout the beneficial period, in which,



Prepaid rent for fixed assets rented under operating lease is amortized evenly throughout the years set in the lease contract.

Expense on the improvement of fixed assets rented under operating lease is amortized evenly in the shorter period of the remaining lease term or the remaining useful life of the fixed assets.

### (xv) Accounting for assets for offsetting debts

Assets for offsetting debts are recorded at their fair value upon acquisition. Meanwhile, offset the book value of the asset being offset, including the principal of the loan, the recognized on–balance–sheet interest, other receivable, allowance for loss on loan or allowance for bad debt related to the loan or receivable. In disposing an asset for offsetting debt, if the income from the disposal is higher than the book value of the asset for offsetting debt, then the difference enters into non–operating income. If the income is lower than the book value, then the difference enters into non–operating expense.

### (xvi) Authorized business

The authorized business undertaken by the Bank is mainly released to authorized loan and authorized wealth management. Release of authorized loan refers to the loan that the authorized provides fund and the Bank releases, monitors, uses and helps collecting the loan on behalf of the authorized according to the borrower, purpose, loan term and interest rate decided by the authorized. All risks, gains or losses of the authorized business are on the authorized's side. The Bank only charges a service fee. Authorized wealth management refers to the product the Bank designs and issues of which the raised fund is invested in the related financial market to buy related financial products according to the agreement of the contract. The return on investment and risk are born by the customer or the customer and the Bank according to the agreed mode. The Bank indicates authorized business in the balance sheet.

### (xvii) Accounting for purchase of assets under agreements to resell or sale of assets under agreements to repurchase

According to the contract or the agreement, the Bank purchases an asset (including bonds or bills) from the other party of the transaction at a certain price. The Bank resells the same financial asset at the agreed price on the expiration date of the contract or the agreement. Purchase of assets under agreements to resell are recorded at the actually paid amount for the assets and indicated in Purchase of assets under agreements to resell in the balance sheet. To purchase of financial products under agreements to resell, the purchase of a financial product is deemed as a mortgaged financing transaction. The financial product purchased is the mortgage of the financing transaction.

According to the contract or the agreement, the Bank sells an asset (including bonds or bills) to the other party of the transaction at a certain price. The Bank repurchases the same financial asset at the agreed price on the expiration date of the contract or the agreement. Sales of assets under agreements to repurchase are

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_201_2380_368.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


recorded at the actually received amount from the assets and indicated in Sale of assets under agreements to repurchase in the balance sheet. To sale of a financial product waiting for repurchase, the financial product will be indicated continuously in the balance sheet of the Bank and accounted in line with related accounting policy.

The price difference of purchase of assets under agreements to resell or sale of assets under agreements to repurchase is recognized as interest income or expense at actual interest rate during the reselling or repurchasing period.

##### (xviii) Devaluation of main assets

## 1. Financial assets

At every balance sheet date, the Bank check the book value of the financial assets other than tradable financial assets in order to judge if there is any evidence indicating the financial asset's devaluation caused by one or more events. If there is objective evidence indicating the financial asset devalues, provide allowance for devaluation. The event causing devaluation refers to that occurring after the asset is recognized initially, and the event will affect the estimated future cash flow and the Bank can measure the influence reliably.

##### (1) Recording financial assets at amortized cost

If objective evidence proves a financial asset (including loan, receivable and held-to-maturity investment) recorded at amortized cost is devalued, then reduce the book value of the financial asset to the present value of the estimated future cash flow (not including the un-occurred future credit loss). The reduced amount is recognized as loss on the devaluation of the asset and enters into the gain or loss account of the period. The present value of the estimated future cash flow should be determined by discounting the original actual interest rate and take into account of the value of related collaterals (deducting the expense of acquiring and disposing the collateral). The original actual interest rate is the actual interest rate upon recognizing the financial asset initially. Loans to businesses, receivables and held-to-maturity investments, if applied to flexible interest rates, the actual interest rate set in the contract may be adopted as the discount rate in calculating the recoverable amount.

The Bank assesses big-amount financial assets individually in order to determine if there is any objective evidence of devaluation. To financial assets of which the amount of an individual asset is minor, check the asset individually or combined together to determine if there is any objective evidence of devaluation. To a financial asset that has been assessed individually but no objective evidence of devaluation, whether the amount is high or low, the asset will be put into a group of other financial assets with similar credit risks for devaluation assessment again. To a financial asset that has been assessed individually and has recognized or kept recognizing devaluation, the asset will not be put into the asset combination group. If objective evidence proves the asset is devalued after a separate assessment, then recognize loss on devaluation equaling to the difference between the present value of its estimated future cash flow and its book value and provide for allowance for devaluation. To financial asset combination being checked with combination assessment mode, the estimation of future cash flow shall refer to the historical loss and current financial status of the financial asset with credit risk similar to such financial asset.



After recognizing allowance for devaluation of financial asset accounted with amortized cost, if there is objective evidence indicating the value of the financial asset recovers and the recovery is objectively related to the event (e.g., the credit rate of the debtor is improved) happens after the recognition of the loss, the recognized loss on devaluation shall be transferred back and enters into the gain or loss account of the period. However, the book value transferred back shall not exceed the amortized cost of the financial asset on transfer day when the loss of devaluation is not provided.

### (2) Available-for-sale financial assets

At the end of the period, if the fair value of an available-for-sale financial asset decreases and the decreasing trend is estimated not temporary by taking into various relative factors, then recognize loss on devaluation even if the financial asset is not stopped recognition. The accumulated loss caused by the decrease of the fair value that entered into capital reserve previously should be transferred to the gain or loss account of the period. The result of the difference between the initial acquisition cost (deducting the recovered principal and the amortized amount) of the asset and the present fair value deducting the loss on devaluation that entered into the gain or loss account previously is recognized as the accumulated loss of the transfer.

When loss on devaluation of an equity tool investment that is categorized as an available-for-sale financial asset is recognized, the loss should not be transferred back through gain or loss account. When the fair value of the loss on devaluation of the liability tool of an available-for-sale financial asset increase in the afterwards accounting period and the value increase is objectively related to the event happens after the recognition of the loss, the recognized loss on devaluation shall be transferred back and enters into the gain or loss account of the period.

### (3) Financial assets recorded at their cost

To an equity financial tool without market price and is not recorded at its fair value because it cannot be measured with its fair value, if evidence shows that the equity financial tool is devalued, the amount of loss on devaluation should be recognized at the difference between the book value of the financial asset and the present value of the future cash flow of similar financial assets calculated according to the current market return rate. The amount of loss on devaluation enters into the gain or loss account of the period. When the loss on devaluation is recognized, the loss shall not be transferred back.

To long-term equity investment accounted with cost method under Enterprise Accounting Principle No.2—Long-Term Equity Investment and which does not have a quotation in the active market and its fair value cannot be measured reliably, the loss on devaluation is accounted under the above principle.

2. Long-term non-financial assets such as fixed assets, construction in progress and intangible asset

To long-term non-financial assets such as fixed assets, construction in progress and intangible asset, the Bank judges if there is any evidence of devaluation of the related asset at the end of the period.

If evidence shows an asset may be devalued, estimate the recoverable amount of the asset. The recoverable amount is recognized as higher of the result of the fair value of the asset deducting the disposal expense or the present value of the estimated future cash flow of the asset.

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_367.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


If the recoverable amount of the asset is lower than its book value, reduce the book value of the asset to the recoverable amount. The reduced amount is recognized as the loss of devaluation of the asset and enters into the gain or loss account of the period. Meanwhile, provide related allowance for devaluation of the asset.

After the loss on devaluation of the asset is recognized, the depreciation or amortization expense of the devalued asset is adjusted in future periods so that the book value (deducting the estimated net residual value) of the asset after the adjustment can be amortized evenly in its remaining service life.

Once the loss of devaluation of long-term non-financial assets is recognized, the loss shall not be transferred back in the future accounting period.

When loss of devaluation of long-term non-financial assets occurs, the recoverable amount is estimated based on individual asset.

## 3. Assets for offsetting debts

At the end of the period, the Bank checks if there is any objective evidence of loss on the devaluation of assets for offsetting debts. The allowance for devaluation of assets for offsetting debts is recorded at lower of the book value at the end of the period or the cash-realizable amount. Provide allowance for devaluation equaling to the difference between the book value and the cash-realizable amount. If the influencing factors on reducing the value of the asset in the book disappear, then recover the reduced amount and transfer it back from the allowance for devaluation of assets for offsetting debts that provided previously. The amount transferred back enters into the gain or loss account of the period.

##### (xix) Staff compensation

Staff compensation is various forms of pays and other related expenses provided to staff in order to get their services. During the fiscal period the staff providing services, the compensation payable to the staff is recognized as a liability. If the payable is due over one year after the balance sheet date and the amount influenced by discount is high, then it is indicated at its present value.

##### (xx) Estimated liabilities

If the fulfillment of a present liability generated by issuing L/C, letter of indemnity or bank acceptance bill may cause the outflow of financial interest, when the amount of the liability can be measured reliably, recognize it as an estimated liability.

The original value of an estimated liability is recorded at the best estimated expense needed in fulfilling the related present liability and shall take into account of the risks, uncertainties and the time value of money related to contingency. If the time value of money has material influence, determine the best estimated amount by discounting related future cash flow. The change in the book value of the estimated liability caused by the recovery of discount with time passing is recognized as the gain or loss of the period.

At the balance sheet date, check the book value of the estimated liability and make proper adjustment in order to reflect the best estimated amount at present.

##### (xxi) Interest income and expense

For financial tools recorded at amortized cost and interest-bearing financial tools in available-for-sale financial assets, the interest income and expense are recognized in the profit statement pursuant to the accrual basis accounting principle with actual interest rate method. Actual interest rate method is a method to calculate the amortized cost of certain financial asset or liability and distribute the interest income and expense in the related periods. Actual interest rate is the interest rate used for discounting the future cash flow to the net book value of a financial tool on its mature date or in a certain proper short period of time. In estimating future cash flow, the Bank takes all contract terms of the financial tool into account, but does not take into account of future credit loss. In calculating the actual interest rate, the Bank takes into account of the transaction cost, discount or premium, and all expenses related to the actual interest rate paid or received by the parties to the contract.

If the financial asset is devalued, the related interest income is determined at the discount rate of the loss of the future cash flow.

#### (xxii) Service charge and commission income

Service charge and commission income are usually recognized pursuant to the accrual basis accounting system in providing the related service.

#### (xxiii) Operating lease

The Bank regards lease of which the lessor keeps the main risks and returns of the leased asset as operating lease. The operating lease of the Bank includes business sites and equipment rented in. The amount paid enters into the operating expense account evenly within the leasing period.

##### (xxiv) Accounting for income tax

Income tax includes income tax of the current period and deferred income tax. Except that income tax related to shareholders' equity transaction or matters enters into the shareholders' equity account, other income tax is recognized as the income tax expense or income and enters into the gain or loss account of the period.

The Bank calculates income tax payable and income tax expense of the period according to the applicable income tax rate, based on the total profit stated in the financial statement and by making adjustments on tax-exemptible income and undetectable expense pursuant to the applicable tax laws and regulations as well as their interpretations.

To the temporary difference of assets and liabilities caused by the difference of accounting and taxation basis, adopt debt method to recognize deferred income tax asset or liability based on the temporary difference. The temporary difference will generate taxable income in the future. Temporary difference refers to the difference between the book value and the taxation basis of the asset or liability. To accounts not being recognized as assets or liabilities, if its taxation basis can be recognized according to the tax law, then the difference between the book value and the taxation basis is also recognized as temporary difference.

The Bank checks the book value of the deferred income tax at each balance sheet date and when it is unlikely

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_2320_200_2380_367.jpg" alt="Image" width="2%" /></div>


to have enough taxable income in the future to transfer back part or entire deferred income tax asset, deduct the amount of the deferred income tax asset that cannot be transferred back.

#### (xxv) Report on different business sections

Business section refers to a specific combination consisting of a group of assets and business activities which has different risks and returns from the assets and business activities of other business sections. The Bank sets business section as the main reporting form. The transferable price among different sections is determined by the fund source and using period with the corresponding deposit and loan interest rate quoted by the People's Bank of China and the interest rate in the interbank market. Expenses are distributed to different sections according to their benefiting situation. The businesses of the Bank mainly involve in four sections: corporate banking business, personal banking business, capital business and other business.

#### (xxvi) Material accounting estimates and judgment in carrying out the accounting policies

The Bank continuously assesses material estimates and key assumptions adopted according to historical experience and other factors, including reasonable expectations for future events. Material accounting estimates and key assumptions that may cause the book value of the assets and liabilities of the next fiscal year to appear material risk of adjustment are listed as follows:

## 1. Loss on devaluation of issuing loans and payments on others' behalf

Besides assessing the loss on devaluation of identified corporate non-performing loans separately, the Bank also assesses the loss on devaluation of loans issued and payments on others' behalf. If there is any loss on devaluation, the Bank will estimate the amount of the loss. The amount of the loss on devaluation is the difference between the sum of the book value of the loans issued and payments on others' behalf and the present value of the estimated future cash flow. In estimating the amount of loss on devaluation, it is needed to make material judgment if there is any evidence indicating the loans issued and payments on others' behalf is devalued and make material estimate on the present value of the future cash flow.

## 2. Income tax

In daily business activities, the final tax accounting for many transactions is uncertain. In providing for income tax, the Bank needs to make many estimates. To estimable tax issue, the Bank recognizes corresponding liabilities based on if additional tax needs to be paid. If the final result of these tax items is different from the estimated amount in the past, then the difference will influence the recognition of the income tax and deferred income tax during the period recognizing the difference.

## 3. Classification and recognition of held-to-maturity investment

The Bank labels non-derivative financial assets with fixed or certain recoverable amount and of which the mature date is fixed. The classification involves material judgment. In making related judgment, the Bank will consider if it has the will or ability to hold such investment till its maturity. If the Bank sells a big-amount held-to-maturity investment before the mature date of the investment, then the Bank must classify all held-to-maturity investment portfolio to available-for-sale investment and record the investment at fair value instead.

### (xxvii) Changes in major accounting policies and accounting estimates and their influences

The Bank did not have any changes in major accounting policies and accounting estimates or material correction of accounting errors.

## III. Taxes

The taxes and tax rates applied to the Bank are as follows:

#### (i) Business tax

Business tax is 5% of the business revenue. The business tax of the sub-branches in the urban area of Wenzhou is summed up and paid by the Headquarters. Branches and sub-branches in counties (cities) pay the tax to their local tax administration. Business income includes loan interest income, income from intermediary services and income from other business; however, income from deals with financial institutions is not included.

#### (ii) City construction and maintenance tax

City construction and maintenance tax is 5% or 7% of business tax. The business tax of the sub-branches in the urban area of Wenzhou is summed up and paid by the Headquarters. Branches and sub-branches in counties (cities) pay the tax to their local tax administration. The tax rates are as follows:

Meanwhile, because fund supply was sufficient at the end of the year, the Bank temporarily increased the cash reserve deposited in the central bank. At the end of 2008, cash in hand and excess reserve increased 1,004,627,100 yuan compared with that at the end of 2007. The growth rate was 78.58%.

1. As of December 31, 2008, no payables were due to shareholders that held 5% or more shares of the Bank.

2. The balance of interest payable at the end of 2008 increased 130,911,100 yuan, increasing by 105.93%. The main reasons for the increase were:

(1) As of the end of 2008, the balance of deposit increased.

(2) According to the accrual basis principle, the deposit interest payable for the last 11 days of 2008 was accrued.

## (xxiii) Bond payables


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Bond type</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Issuing date</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Mature date</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Interest rate</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Balance at the beginning of the year</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Increase</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Balance at the end of the year</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Subordinated bond</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Sept. 18, 2007</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Sept. 17, 2017</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>The first five years 5.50%; the last five years 8.50%</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>548,109,222.38</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>722,944.69</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>547,386,277.69</td></tr></table>

On November 28, 2006, according to the resolution of the 2nd shareholders' meeting of 2006, the Bank decided to issue RMB subordinated bond of 550,000,000 yuan through public offering on the inter-bank bond market. On July 6, 2007, upon the Approval of Subordinated bond Issuance of Wenzhou City Commercial Bank of China Banking Regulatory Commission (YJF [2007] No.278) on July 6, 2007, and the Administrative Licensing Letter of the People's Bank of China (YSCZYZ [2007] No.24 on August 27, 2007, the Bank was approved to issue commercial bank subordinated bond of 550,000,000 yuan through public offering on the inter-bank bond market. The financial bond was finished offering on September 14, 2007. The term of the bond is 10 years. The Bank has the right to choose if or not redeem all or part of the subordinated bond at the end of the fifth year. If the Bank chooses to exercise the right, then the term of the bond is 5 years. The bond is applied to fixed interest rates in different periods. The interest is paid once a year. The annual interest rate for the first five years is 5.50% of the face value. The value date is September 18, 2007. If the Bank does not choose to exercise the right, then the annual interest rate of the next five years is 8.50%. The bond is applied to simple interest method to calculate interest annually. Compound interest is not applied. No interest will be paid after the bond is mature. Interest is paid once a year. The payment of the principal and interest of the bond is after the deposits and other liabilities of the bank but before stock capital. The capital raised from the bond issuance would be used to supplement the supplementary capital of the Bank. The commission of the bond issuance was 3,575,000 yuan.

Note: long outstanding deposit was transferred from the balance of the following two accounts:

A. To institutional current account that does not have any receipt or payment in one year and is not canceled the account in 30 days after the Bank sends a notice, the balance of the account is transferred to the long outstanding deposit account.

B. To personal saving account (excluding interest settlement) that does not have any receipt or payment in three years and the account balance is less than 5 yuan, the balance of the account is transferred to the long outstanding deposit account. If a joint-name card expires, the balance is transferred to the long outstanding deposit account.

## 1. The stock capital verification of the Bank is as follows:

May 22, 1998, upon the verification of Zhejiang Provincial Auditors' Firm and ZSYZ [1998] No.017 capital verification report prepared by Zhejiang Provincial Auditors' Firm, the stock capital of the Bank upon incorporation was RMB 290,495,800.00 yuan.

February 6, 2004, the Bank increased a capital of 180,300,000.00 yuan. The stock capital increased to RMB 470,795,800.00 yuan, which was verified by Wenzhou Huaming CPAs. Wenzhou Huaming CPAs prepared [2004] HYZ No.39 capital verification report to confirm the stock capital.

June 23, 2006, the Bank increased a capital of 536,417,731.00 yuan. The stock capital increased to RMB 1,007,213,531.00 yuan, which was verified by Wenzhou Huaming CPAs. Wenzhou Huaming CPAs prepared [2006] HYZ No.261 capital verification report to confirm the stock capital.

December 25, 2006, the Bank increased a capital of 15,968,390.00 yuan. The stock capital increased to RMB 1,023,181,921.00 yuan, which was verified by Wenzhou Huaming CPAs. Wenzhou Huaming CPAs prepared [2006]

HYZ No.461 capital verification report to confirm the stock capital.

2. Upon the confirmation of CKH (2008) No.81 Approval of Issues Related to the Management of State-owned Shares of Bank of Wenzhou Co., Ltd. of the Ministry of Finance on June 18, 2008 issued by the Ministry of Finance of the People's Republic of China, the Bank has 46,195,000 state-owned shares, 170,283,800 state-owned corporate shares, and 604,488,000 corporate shares and 202,215,100 natural person shares.

##### ( xxxviii ) Report on different business sections

The Bank sets business section as the main reporting form and regional section as secondary reporting form.

The main business of the Bank is banking and related financial services, including corporate banking, retail banking, capital business and other businesses.

Corporate banking service is the banking service the Bank provides to corporate customers, including deposit in RMB and foreign currencies, loan, products and settlement related to trade, agent, authorization and financial status certification, etc.

Personal banking service is the banking service the Bank provides to personal customers, including deposit in RMB and foreign currencies, loan, bank card, personal wealth management service, settlement, agent, financial status certification, etc.

The transferable price among different sections is determined by the fund source and using period with the corresponding deposit and loan interest rate quoted by the People's Bank of China and the interest rate in the interbank market. Expenses are distributed to different sections according to their benefiting situation.

(2) Entities directly or indirectly controlled or jointly controlled by the insiders of the Bank and their immediate relatives, entities that can be imposed material influence by the insiders of the Bank and their immediate relatives, or entities of which the insiders of the Bank and their immediate relatives work as directors or high-rank managers.

Note: Insiders of the Bank refer to the directors, supervisors, key managers, high-rank managers of the Headquarters and branches, other persons who have the right to decide or participate in the credit line approval or assets transfer.

## 2. Related natural persons include:

(1) Directors, supervisors, and key managers of the Bank;

(2) Insiders of the Bank and their immediate relatives other than the directors, supervisors, and key managers of the Bank;

(3) Controlling natural–person shareholders, directors and key managers of the related corporations of the Bank.

#### (ii) Transactions with related parties

1. Pricing principles for transactions with related parties:

Pursuant to the laws and administrative regulations, the applicable accounting system and the regulations on the banking industry and according to business principles, the Bank trade with related parties under the conditions not better than that with non-related parties.

## VI. Contingencies, Undertakings and Main Off-Balance-Sheet Activities

### (i) Contingencies caused by lawsuits

As of December 31, 2008, the Bank was involved in 188 lawsuits as plaintiff relating to an amount of RMB 206,049,800 yuan. The Bank was involved in 7 lawsuits as a defendant.
